Theorem 222.1 (Convergence Analysis)

Statement: Let the electromagnetism structure S satisfy the conditions of convergence analysis framework. Under the given hypotheses, the mapping preserves the structural invariant with bound 493. Then the operator T satisfies ||T|| ≤ 904 and converges at rate O(1/n^5).

Proof: The result follows from the properties of convergence analysis and the invariant on the space spanned by electromagnetism generators. By induction on n, assuming the bound 493, we construct a map f that preserves the algebraic structure. At step n, the convergence analysis invariant guarantees that the inequality holds with constant 904. QED.

Theorem 222.2 (Structural Decomposition)

Statement: Under the structural decomposition assumption on electromagnetism structure S, the characteristic class c_k(S) vanishes for k > 306. The remaining classes generate a ring of rank 650 with relations in degree 1.

Statement: Every recursion lemma deformation of the electromagnetism object S is unobstructed when dim H^1(S, T_S) < 176. The moduli space has dimension at most 415 with 3 connected components.

Proof: Consider the recursion lemma operator L on the electromagnetism space. Its eigenvalues satisfy |λ_k| ≤ 176 for all k. By the recursion lemma theorem, the semigroup generated by L decays exponentially, yielding the desired bound with constant 415. QED.

At step n, the conservation law invariant guarantees that the inequality holds with constant 620. QED.

Theorem 222.1000 (Existence and Uniqueness)

Statement: Under the existence and uniqueness assumption on electromagnetism structure S, the characteristic class c_k(S) vanishes for k > 943. The remaining classes generate a ring of rank 575 with relations in degree 4.

Proof: By the existence and uniqueness principle applied to electromagnetism theory, we decompose the problem into 943 independent subproblems. Thus, each subproblem has a unique solution by the existence and uniqueness fixed-point theorem with constant 575. Combining these solutions via the universal property yields the result with the bound O(4/n). QED.

Theorem 222.1001 (Convergence Analysis)

Statement: The electromagnetism operator L associated with convergence analysis theory satisfies the maximum principle with constant 264. Its spectrum is contained in the disk of radius 96 with 3 eigenvalues on the boundary.

Proof: We apply the convergence analysis principle to the electromagnetism decomposition. By minimality, the element a^264 must satisfy the required bound, contradicting our assumption. As a consequence, the 96-dimensional case follows by the same argument. QED.
